--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (R and P), let's follow the evaluation process:

1. **Examine the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are R and P.

2. **Examine the immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of R are F.
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of P are A, I, and Q.

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - F (neighbor of R) connects to B, C, and O.
   - B (neighbor of F) connects to S, E, C, T, and P.
   - C (neighbor of F) connects to T, G, N, H, and B.
   - O (neighbor of F) connects to G and T.
   - S (neighbor of B) connects to E.
   - E (neighbor of B and S) connects to B.
   - T (neighbor of B and C) connects to O, H, and N.
   - G (neighbor of C) connects to H.
   - N (neighbor of C and T) connects to H.
   - H (neighbor of C, T, and G) connects to N.
   - A (neighbor of P) connects to S.
   - Q (neighbor of P) connects to J.
   - I (neighbor of P) connects to Q and J.
   - J (neighbor of Q and I) connects to Q.

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - The paths from R to P go through F to B to P.
   - Another path goes through F to C to T to H to N to C to B to P.
   - There is no other direct path from Q or I to R.

5. **Check for bottlenecks:**
   - The edges (F, B) and (B, P) are critical as they form a direct path.
   - Removing (F, B) or (B, P) will break one direct path, but we need to ensure all paths are broken.

6. **Check if the removal of one edge suffices:**
   - Removing (F, B) will not suffice as there is another path from F to C to T to H to N to C to B.
   - Removing (B, P) will not suffice as there is still another path through F to C to T to H to N to C to B.

7. **Compare if the removal of edges connected to either one of the black nodes results in a smaller set of edges to be removed:**
   - Removing (F, B) and (F, C) will break all paths from R to P.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes is 2.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(F, B), (F, C)]

   - The immediate neighbors of Q are G, O, and P.

   - From H to G to Q.
   - From H to J to G to Q.
   - From H to I to J to G to Q.

   - Removing the edges from H to both I and its neighbors J and G to any other nodes can leave H isolated.
   - Similarly, removing edges that directly link back up to Q, i.e., G to O, O to P and then P to Q is the quickest way.

4. **Determine the minimal cut-set:**
   - After examining the paths, we see that removing the edge between H to I and G to P completely disconnects H and Q.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed is:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(H, I), (G, P)].

2. The immediate neighboring nodes of J are I, H, G, and C.
3. The immediate neighboring nodes of R are N, O, and C.
4. The nodes around J and R form a central hub structure:
   - J connects to I, H, G, and C.
   - R connects to N, O, and C.
   - We see that C connects to G, J, and R, thus acting as a central hub.

5. To completely disconnect J and R, one option is to remove the connections from C to both J and R. We consider all paths connecting J and R:
   - Path 1 via C: (J, C, R)
   - Path 2 via B: (J, G, B, S, E, F, T, B, R)
   - Path 3: (I, H, G, B, S, E, F, T, B, R)

6. All paths either go via common nodes, especially through C, showing C as a bottleneck.
7. By removing the connections from C to G, G to B, and J to C, all paths through these nodes are no longer viable, disconnecting J and R from each other.

8. This results in three edges that need to be removed. Checking smaller sets results in paths still connecting J and R via intermediate nodes:
   - Removing only (C, J) does not prevent J from reaching R in several other paths, among which (J, G, B, S) or other combinations leading to R.
   - Removing either (C, G) or (C, R) also doesn't suffice, for similar reasons.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges that must be removed for complete disconnection is three:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(C, G), (G, B), (C, J)].
